- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 9
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 4
        - Boldness: 7

        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 4
        - Meanness: 7
        - Neediness: 8
        - Chattiness: 2
        - DeceptiveBias: 6
        - Rationale: Increasing VictoryCompetitiveness (8→9) in response to Greece capturing Damascus and becoming a direct domination competitor. While we have a Defensive Pact, I must be more vigilant about competitors pursuing the same victory path. Maintaining aggressive military posture with Boldness 7 and WarBias 6, while keeping strategic flexibility. The hostile diplomatic environment (Egypt and Byzantium denounced me, Egypt sees me as existential threat) requires sustained competitive pressure.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Martial Spirit (Policy)
        - Rationale: Martial Spirit provides +25% combat strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns - perfect timing for the final push toward Domination Victory. This will significantly boost tank and infantry assaults on the remaining Iroquois capitals (Onondaga and Amsterdam). With policy adoption 14 turns away, this bonus will be active during the critical endgame battles. The doubled razing speed and reduced war weariness are additional benefits for sustained offensive operations. This Level 2 Autocracy tenet synergizes perfectly with the current military-focused strategy.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Egypt has denounced me and views my warmongering as an existential threat. They are the logical next target for conquest - they have 11 cities, already hostile relations, and are positioned between my territories. Setting negative relationship values to prepare for eventual conflict while building up forces after consolidating Arabian conquests. Egypt also has a Defensive Pact with The Iroquois, requiring careful timing.
        - UpdatedTurn: 236
        
        ### Greece
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Greece relationship to neutral (from -15/-20) now that they've declared war and broken our Defensive Pact. They attacked ME, not vice versa. Removing artificial constraints to let in-game AI handle active war naturally. I'm not seeking Greece's total destruction - Egypt remains the primary post-war target as the logical conquest path with 11 cities and existing hostility. Greece's betrayal was their choice; I'll defend vigorously but remain open to eventual peace terms.
        - UpdatedTurn: 243
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: The Iroquois just declared war on me (Turn 267) and represent a major threat with 10 cities, 1995 military strength, and control of 2 capitals (Onondaga + Amsterdam). They have a Defensive Pact with Egypt and are proposing to sanction me in the World Congress. Setting hostile relationship modifiers to prepare for inevitable conflict after finishing Greece/Byzantium. They're the final obstacle to domination victory with their 2 capitals.
        - UpdatedTurn: 267
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: The Netherlands declared war on me Turn 280, joining The Iroquois and Egypt in active hostilities. With 7 cities, 837 military strength, and multiple allied city-states (Okilis, Harappa, Utica), they represent a significant coastal threat. Setting hostile relationship modifiers to prepare the AI for sustained conflict. However, they're a secondary target - my primary objectives remain capturing Onondaga, Amsterdam, and Thebes for domination victory. The Netherlands will likely seek peace once I've shattered their allied city-states and demonstrated overwhelming force.
        - UpdatedTurn: 281
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Maintaining current Conquest strategy with no adjustments needed. Montreal siege progressing excellently at 56% health after sustained bombardment. This turn eliminated 7+ Iroquois units including submarines and destroyers, demonstrating the effectiveness of current force composition. Advanced Ballistics completes this turn, enabling nuclear breakthrough operations against heavily fortified capitals Onondaga and Amsterdam. Current flavor balance (Offense 80, Air 75, Nuke/UseNuke 80) is optimal for combined arms operations. Mobile Tactics research on track to provide Mechanized Infantry for exploitation phase. With 62% domination progress and clear momentum, no strategic changes warranted - continue systematic destruction of Iroquois forces and advance toward remaining capitals.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Open Door: Vilnius:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Greek Sphere of Influence: Aztlan:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - Open Door: Shanghai:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 381
          - German Sphere of Influence: Lutetia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 381
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: The Iroquois
          - Repeal: Open Door: Vilnius:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Germany
          - Enact: Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - Proposer: Sweden

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Stealth
        - Rationale: Stealth bomber unlocks are critical for late-game siege operations and securing air supremacy as we push toward the remaining Iroquois capitals (Amsterdam and Onondaga) and eventually Egypt's capital (Thebes). This will allow us to bypass defenses and strike deep into enemy territory with zero risk of interception.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Reaffirming Martial Spirit as our next policy. The +25% attack strength for melee units, doubled razing speed, and 25% reduction in war weariness are essential for our final push to capture the remaining three original capitals and secure a Domination victory.
        - Policy: Martial Spirit
    - #2:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Maintaining our highly optimized Conquest strategy and flavors. Our double-pincer movement is squeezing the Iroquois from both East and West. Montreal is on the verge of falling, and our air force is perfectly rebased to support the advance on Amsterdam and Onondaga. No strategic adjustments are necessary as we maintain complete military and diplomatic dominance.

    - inputTokens: 117110
    - reasoningTokens: 6738
    - outputTokens: 228
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current State**
            
            My current focus is on a comprehensive review of our position. I am meticulously examining our dominance progress, resource allocation, ongoing policy implementations, technological advancements, diplomatic standings, and the broader geopolitical landscape to identify immediate strategic imperatives.
            
            **Assessing Our Progress**
            
            I'm assessing our current standing. We've achieved 62% capital control and just entered Era 7 with a strong science lead. Our cultural dominance is also significant, though diplomatic victory is tight. Battles around Montreal are proving fruitful, and our naval presence is expanding.
            
            **Revising Naval Strategy**
            
            Despite recent losses of Battleships to Iroquois submarines and destroyers, we retaliated effectively, destroying their subs and destroyers. We're bolstering defenses with Military Bases and training air units, but our negative gold per turn demands immediate attention.
            
            **Selecting Next Technology**
            
            I am prioritizing our next technological advancement, weighing the strategic benefits of either Refrigeration or Stealth. My analysis indicates Stealth offers a more immediate tactical advantage for our late-game objectives, particularly with the potent Stealth Bomber unit. I will select Stealth for immediate research.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I am considering our next policy selection. While 'Martial Spirit' offers excellent offensive bonuses and reduced war weariness, I'm evaluating if other Autocracy tenets might better suit our current economic challenges or long-term goals, particularly as we approach turn 397.
            
            **Focusing on Montreal**
            
            I am consolidating our forces around Montreal. With multiple tanks and paratroopers in position, and bombers rebasing to support the offensive, I'm confident in our push towards the city itself. Our air superiority is key.
            
            **Positioning Air Assets**
            
            I'm repositioning bombers from Lisht to bases like Hamburg and Helsinki, enabling strikes into Iroquois territory. My focus is now on coordinating these air assets for maximum impact against their core cities, leveraging our proximity from European bases.
            
            **Executing Pincer Movement**
            
            I'm orchestrating a massive double envelopment of the Iroquois from both East and West. With overwhelming modern armor and rocket artillery poised, their empire is strategically flanked. Egypt's defensive pact with them complicates future moves post-treaty.
            
            **Clarifying Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm re-examining the domination victory requirements. It appears controlling or vassalizing original capitals is key, and I've identified that we currently hold five of the eight, with a clear path to potentially vassalizing Greece.
            
            **Securing Domination Victory**
            
            I'm finalizing the path to domination. With five capitals already secured, my immediate objective is to capture Amsterdam and Onondaga. Following that, I will assess Egypt's defensive pact and strategy to secure their capital and achieve victory.
            
            **Preparing for Endgame**
            
            My attention is now on the looming peace treaty expiration with Egypt. I am preparing to seize Thebes, anticipating their potential aggression. Our hostile diplomatic standings with Egypt, Iroquois, and the Netherlands are advantageous for this planned offensive. Greece remains a loyal, albeit unhappy, vassal.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I am confirming our next policy. 'Martial Spirit' is locked in for its significant combat and war weariness benefits, perfectly suiting our aggressive endgame. I am also ensuring our Grand Strategy remains focused on Conquest, with current flavor distributions deemed optimal.
            
            **Adjusting Resource Management**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our economic situation. The current -65 gold per turn is unsustainable, likely due to our large military size and the "Global Peace Accords" resolution impacting unit maintenance costs. I need to find ways to improve our gold generation immediately.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Posture**
            
            I'm reassessing our diplomatic stance, particularly concerning Egypt and the Iroquois. The upcoming peace treaty expiration with Egypt, coupled with ongoing hostilities with the Iroquois, demands careful consideration of our relationships and potential warmonger penalties given the active "Global Peace Accords" resolution.
            
            **Confirming Target Status**
            
            I've confirmed the Netherlands is out of the game, simplifying my diplomatic assessment. My focus is now on Egypt and the Iroquois, as our peace treaty with Egypt expires soon, and we're already at war with the Iroquois. The remaining majors are my vassals.
            
            **Controlling World Congress**
            
            I've confirmed our overwhelming control of the World Congress, commanding 42 delegates. This allows us to dictate policy, as seen with the upcoming Passport System proposal. I’m prioritizing securing the repeal of Open Door: Vilnius and blocking Iroquois tourism initiatives.
            
            **Passing Passport System**
            
            I am now focusing on the World Congress, specifically the Passport System. While initially considering its repeal, I realize a 50% tourism boost significantly accelerates our path to a cultural victory, complementing our domination efforts. I will push to pass this proposal.
            
            **Executing Pincer**
            
            My forces are poised for a decisive pincer movement, ready to crush the Iroquois after Montreal's imminent fall. My eastern and western armies will converge, swiftly clearing their remaining cities and securing domination. I'm also monitoring Egyptian units near Hong Kong.
            
            **Improving Gold Generation**
            
            I am analyzing our current gold deficit, which stems from high unit maintenance costs exacerbated by the "Global Peace Accords" resolution. While our AI is managing trade routes effectively, I will explore additional trade deals and potential gold acquisition from our vassals to alleviate the strain and prevent science penalties.
            
            **Improving Gold Per Turn**
            
            My focus is on improving our gold per turn, which is currently negative due to high unit maintenance. I'm analyzing incoming gold from vassals and trade deals, noting the impending loss of the Egypt treaty's gold. I will ensure the AI continues to prioritize gold generation and trade.
            
            **Researching and Finalizing**
            
            I've confirmed `Stealth` as our next technology, as it offers immediate tactical advantages in Era 7. `Martial Spirit` is locked in as our next policy, perfectly aligning with our aggressive endgame. My current strategy remains Conquest, with optimal flavor distributions.
            
            **Finalizing Endgame Strategy**
            
            My research into `Stealth` is confirmed for its tactical impact on bomber units against remaining Iroquois capitals and Egypt. `Martial Spirit` is locked for its combat and war weariness benefits, reinforcing our Conquest Grand Strategy and aggressive endgame. No tool calls needed at this juncture.
